> DEVELOPER

whats teh password after reimnstll?

> AGENT

From the installer design: the DB password is **preserved** after a reinstall.

- On fresh install: a random password is generated and saved to `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/postgres/.secrets`
- On reinstall: the password is **reused** from `.secrets` so it matches the existing postgres data directory
- This prevents authentication failures when reinstalling with an existing database

So after a reinstall, the password is the same as before — you can find it in `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/postgres/.secrets`.

> DEVELOPER

and the user?

> AGENT

The database user is `sandcastle` — that's the PostgreSQL username used by the Rails app. It's consistent across installs and not randomly generated.

> AGENT

The form uses `form_with url:` without a model object, so fields are submitted as top-level params (`email`, `message`) instead of nested under `invite`. Fix by using `model:` or adding the scope explicitly:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01BPSMaNLvXbBS56G4FLJXVJ
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/admin/invites/index.html.erb",
  "old_string": "      <%= form_with url: admin_invites_path, method: :post, class: \"space-y-4\" do |form| %>",
  "new_string": "      <%= form_with url: admin_invites_path, method: :post, scope: :invite, class: \"space-y-4\" do |form| %>"
}
```

> AGENT

Adding `scope: :invite` makes Rails nest the fields as `invite[email]` and `invite[message]`, which is what `params.expect(invite: [...])` requires.

```
    59→    function setStepActive(id) {
    60→      var el = document.getElementById(id);
    61→      if (!el) return;
    62→      el.querySelector(".step-icon").className = "step-icon w-5 h-5 rounded-full bg-blue-500 flex items-center justify-center";
    63→      el.querySelector(".step-icon").innerHTML = '<svg class="w-3 h-3 text-white animate-spin" fill="none" viewBox="0 0 24 24"><circle class="opacity-25" cx="12" cy="12" r="10" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="4"></circle><path class="opacity-75" fill="currentColor" d="M4 12a8 8 0 018-8V0C5.373 0 0 5.373 0 12h4z"></path></svg>';
    64→      el.querySelector(".step-text").className = "step-text text-gray-700 font-medium";
    65→    }
    66→
    67→    function setStepDone(id) {
    68→      var el = document.getElementById(id);
    69→      if (!el) return;
    70→      el.querySelector(".step-icon").className = "step-icon w-5 h-5 rounded-full bg-green-500 flex items-center justify-center";

    72→      el.querySelector(".step-text").className = "step-text text-green-700 font-medium";
    73→    }
    74→
    75→    function updateUI(data) {
    76→      if (data.status === "starting") {
    77→        setStepActive("step-starting");
    78→      } else if (data.status === "waiting_for_url") {
    79→        setStepDone("step-starting");
    80→        setStepActive("step-url");
    81→      } else if (data.status === "login_ready") {
    82→        setStepDone("step-starting");
    83→        setStepDone("step-url");
    84→        setStepActive("step-auth");
    85→        var linkDiv = document.getElementById("login-link");
    86→        var linkEl = document.getElementById("login-url");

    88→          linkEl.href = data.login_url;
    89→          linkDiv.classList.remove("hidden");
    90→        }
    91→      } else if (data.status === "authenticated") {
    92→        setStepDone("step-starting");
    93→        setStepDone("step-url");
    94→        setStepDone("step-auth");
    95→        clearInterval(poll);
    96→        window.location.reload();
    97→      } else if (data.status === "error") {
    98→        clearInterval(poll);
    99→        var errDiv = document.getElementById("login-error");
   100→        if (errDiv) {
   101→          errDiv.querySelector("p").textContent = data.error;
   102→          errDiv.classList.remove("hidden");
   103→        }
   104→      }
   105→      currentStatus = data.status;
   106→    }
   107→
   108→    var poll = setInterval(function() {
   109→      fetch("<%= login_status_tailscale_path %>", { headers: { "Accept": "application/json" } })
   110→        .then(function(r) { return r.json(); })
   111→        .then(updateUI)
   112→        .catch(function() {});
   113→    }, 2000);
   114→  })();
   115→</script>
```

```
     1→class Invite < ApplicationRecord
     2→  belongs_to :invited_by, class_name: "User"
     3→
     4→  validates :email, presence: true, format: { with: URI::MailTo::EMAIL_REGEXP }
     5→  validates :token, presence: true, uniqueness: true
     6→
     7→  normalizes :email, with: ->(e) { e.strip.downcase }
     8→
     9→  before_validation :generate_token, on: :create
    10→  before_create :set_default_expiry
    11→
    12→  scope :pending, -> { where(accepted_at: nil).where("expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > ?", Time.current) }
    13→  scope :accepted, -> { where.not(accepted_at: nil) }
    14→  scope :expired, -> { where(accepted_at: nil).where("expires_at IS NOT NULL AND expires_at <= ?", Time.current) }
    15→
    16→  def accepted?
    17→    accepted_at.present?
    18→  end
    19→
    20→  def expired?
    21→    expires_at.present? && expires_at <= Time.current
    22→  end
    23→
    24→  def pending?
    25→    !accepted? && !expired?
    26→  end
    27→
    28→  def status
    29→    if accepted?
    30→      "accepted"
    31→    elsif expired?
    32→      "expired"
    33→    else
    34→      "pending"
    35→    end
    36→  end
    37→
    38→  def accept!
    39→    update!(accepted_at: Time.current)
    40→  end
    41→
    42→  private
    43→
    44→  def generate_token
    45→    self.token = SecureRandom.urlsafe_base64(32)
    46→  end
    47→
    48→  def set_default_expiry
    49→    self.expires_at ||= 7.days.from_now
    50→  end
    51→end
    52→
```

```
     1→class RegistrationsController < ApplicationController
     2→  allow_unauthenticated_access
     3→  before_action :set_invite
     4→
     5→  def new
     6→    @user = User.new(email_address: @invite.email)
     7→  end
     8→
     9→  def create
    10→    @user = User.new(registration_params)
    11→    @user.email_address = @invite.email
    12→
    13→    if @user.save
    14→      @invite.accept!
    15→      start_new_session_for(@user)
    16→      redirect_to root_path, notice: "Welcome to Sandcastle! Your account is now active."
    17→    else
    18→      render :new, status: :unprocessable_entity
    19→    end
    20→  end
    21→
    22→  private
    23→
    24→  def set_invite
    25→    @invite = Invite.find_by(token: params[:token])
    26→
    27→    unless @invite
    28→      redirect_to new_session_path, alert: "Invite link is invalid."
    29→      return
    30→    end
    31→
    32→    if @invite.accepted?
    33→      redirect_to new_session_path, alert: "This invite has already been used."
    34→      return
    35→    end
    36→
    37→    if @invite.expired?
    38→      redirect_to new_session_path, alert: "This invite has expired."
    39→    end
    40→  end
    41→
    42→  def registration_params
    43→    params.expect(user: [ :name, :full_name, :password, :password_confirmation, :ssh_public_key ])
    44→  end
    45→end
    46→
```

```
     6→    <div class="mt-4 p-4 bg-blue-50 rounded-lg border border-blue-200">

    26→    </div>
    27→
    28→    <div class="my-5">

    31→            placeholder: "e.g. johndoe",

    33→      <p class="mt-1 text-xs text-gray-500">Lowercase letters, numbers, hyphens, underscores (2–31 chars, must start with a letter).</p>
    34→    </div>
    35→
    36→    <div class="my-5">

    38→      <%= form.text_field :full_name, autocomplete: "name",

    41→    </div>

    48→    </div>
```

> AGENT

Found the bug: the registration form uses `form_with url:` without `scope: :user`, so params arrive as top-level (same issue as the invite form was). `registration_params` expects them nested under `user`.

Two fixes needed:
1. Add `scope: :user` to the registration form
2. Add "Copy invite link" to the invite list

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01CjtpbQr1BK39kEyPoM6ypm
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/registrations/new.html.erb",
  "old_string": "  <%= form_with url: accept_registration_path(token: params[:token]), method: :post, class: \"contents\" do |form| %>",
  "new_string": "  <%= form_with url: accept_registration_path(token: params[:token]), method: :post, scope: :user, class: \"contents\" do |form| %>"
}
```

> AGENT

Summary of changes:
- **`registrations/new.html.erb`**: Added `scope: :user` so form params are nested as `user[name]`, `user[password]` etc. — this is what `registration_params` expects and was the root cause of the broken invite link
- **`admin/invites/index.html.erb`**: Added "Copy link" button for pending invites that copies the full registration URL to the clipboard and briefly shows "Copied!" confirmation

> AGENT

Done. Summary of changes:

- **`docker-compose.dev.yml`**: Traefik now mounts `./.cert:/certs:ro`, the `Host` rules use `sandcastle.tc`, the `tls.certificates` block in `rails.yml` points to `/certs/cert.pem` and `/certs/key.pem`, and `SANDCASTLE_HOST`/`SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL` are updated to `sandcastle.tc`
- **`.gitignore`**: Added `/.cert/` (`.pem` was already there)

To generate the certs with mkcert:
```bash
mkdir .cert
mkcert -cert-file .cert/cert.pem -key-file .cert/key.pem sandcastle.tc
```
And add `127.0.0.1 sandcastle.tc` to `/etc/hosts`.

```
     1→class SandboxManager
     2→  DATA_DIR = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_DATA_DIR", "/data")
     3→  DEFAULT_IMAGE = "ghcr.io/thieso2/sandcastle-sandbox:latest"
     4→
     5→  class Error < StandardError; end
     6→
     7→  def create(user:, name:, image: DEFAULT_IMAGE, persistent: false, tailscale: false, mount_home: false, data_path: nil, temporary: false)
     8→    # Build sandbox record (not saved yet)
     9→    sandbox = user.sandboxes.build(
    10→      name: name,
    11→      image: image,
    12→      status: "pending",
    13→      persistent_volume: persistent,
    14→      mount_home: mount_home,
    15→      data_path: data_path,
    16→      temporary: temporary
    17→    )
    18→
    19→    if persistent
    20→      sandbox.volume_path = "#{DATA_DIR}/sandboxes/#{sandbox.full_name}/vol"
    21→    end
    22→
    23→    # Validate before doing expensive operations
    24→    sandbox.validate!
    25→
    26→    # Create directories FIRST (can fail fast before saving record)
    27→    ensure_mount_dirs(user, sandbox)
    28→
    29→    # Now safe to save
    30→    sandbox.save!
    31→
    32→    # Pull image
    33→    ensure_image(image)
    34→
    35→    # Create and start container
    36→    create_container_and_start(sandbox: sandbox, user: user)
    37→
    38→    # Connect to Tailscale if requested
    39→    if (tailscale || user.tailscale_auto_connect?) && user.tailscale_enabled?
    40→      TailscaleManager.new.connect_sandbox(sandbox: sandbox)
    41→    end
    42→
    43→    sandbox
    44→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    45→    sandbox&.update(status: "destroyed") if sandbox&.persisted?
    46→    raise Error, "Failed to create container: #{e.message}"
    47→  rescue => e
    48→    # If anything fails before save, no DB record is created
    49→    # If anything fails after save, mark as destroyed
    50→    sandbox&.update(status: "destroyed") if sandbox&.persisted?
    51→    raise Error, e.message
    52→  end
    53→
    54→  # Public method for job usage
    55→  def create_container_and_start(sandbox:, user:)
    56→    container = Docker::Container.create(
    57→      "name" => sandbox.full_name,
    58→      "Image" => sandbox.image,
    59→      "Hostname" => sandbox.full_name,
    60→      "Env" => container_env(user, sandbox),
    61→      "HostConfig" => {
    62→        "Runtime" => container_runtime,
    63→        "PortBindings" => {
    64→          "22/tcp" => [ { "HostPort" => sandbox.ssh_port.to_s } ]
    65→        },
    66→        "Binds" => volume_binds(user, sandbox),
    67→        "RestartPolicy" => { "Name" => "unless-stopped" }
    68→      }
    69→    )
    70→
    71→    container.start
    72→    container.refresh!
    73→    raise Error, "Container failed to start (state: #{container.json.dig("State", "Status")})" unless container.json.dig("State", "Running")
    74→    sandbox.update!(container_id: container.id, status: "running")
    75→  end
    76→
    77→  # Public method for job usage
    78→  def ensure_image(image)
    79→    Docker::Image.create("fromImage" => image)
    80→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    81→    raise Error, "Failed to pull image #{image}: #{e.message}"
    82→  end
    83→
    84→  # Public method for job usage
    85→  def ensure_mount_dirs(user, sandbox)
    86→    # Directories bind-mounted into Sysbox containers must be world-writable
    87→    # because Sysbox maps container root to a high host UID (via /etc/subuid)
    88→    # that won't match the directory owner.
    89→
    90→    # Create BTRFS subvolume for user directory if on BTRFS
    91→    BtrfsHelper.create_user_subvolume(user.name)
    92→
    93→    if sandbox.mount_home
    94→      dir = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/home"
    95→      FileUtils.mkdir_p(dir)
    96→      FileUtils.chmod(0o777, dir)
    97→    end
    98→    if sandbox.data_path.present?
    99→      # Create BTRFS subvolume for data directory if on BTRFS
   100→      BtrfsHelper.create_user_data_subvolume(user.name, sandbox.data_path)
   101→
   102→      dir = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/data/#{sandbox.data_path}".chomp("/")
   103→      FileUtils.mkdir_p(dir)
   104→      FileUtils.chmod(0o777, dir)
   105→    end
   106→    if sandbox.persistent_volume && sandbox.volume_path
   107→      FileUtils.mkdir_p(sandbox.volume_path)
   108→      FileUtils.chmod(0o777, sandbox.volume_path)
   109→    end
   110→    # Chrome profile persistence: mount .config/google-chrome separately if not mounting full home
   111→    if user.chrome_persist_profile? && !sandbox.mount_home
   112→      dir = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/chrome-profile"
   113→      FileUtils.mkdir_p(dir)
   114→      FileUtils.chmod(0o777, dir)
   115→    end
   116→  rescue Errno::EACCES, Errno::ENOENT => e
   117→    raise Error, "Failed to create mount directories: #{e.message}"
   118→  end
   119→
   120→  def destroy(sandbox:, keep_volume: false)
   121→    begin
   122→      TerminalManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
   123→    rescue TerminalManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError
   124→      # best-effort terminal cleanup
   125→    end
   126→
   127→    begin
   128→      VncManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
   129→    rescue VncManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError
   130→      # best-effort VNC cleanup
   131→    end
   132→
   133→    RouteManager.new.remove_all_routes(sandbox: sandbox) if sandbox.routed?
   134→
   135→    if sandbox.tailscale?
   136→      TailscaleManager.new.disconnect_sandbox(sandbox: sandbox)
   137→    end
   138→
   139→    if sandbox.container_id.present?
   140→      begin
   141→        container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   142→        container.stop(t: 5) rescue nil
   143→        container.delete(force: true)
   144→      rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   145→        # Container already gone
   146→      end
   147→    end
   148→
   149→    unless keep_volume
   150→      FileUtils.rm_rf(sandbox.volume_path) if sandbox.volume_path.present?
   151→    end
   152→
   153→    sandbox.update!(status: "destroyed", container_id: nil)
   154→  end
   155→
   156→  def start(sandbox:)
   157→    raise Error, "Sandbox is destroyed" if sandbox.status == "destroyed"
   158→    return sandbox if sandbox.status == "running"
   159→
   160→    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   161→    container.start
   162→    sandbox.update!(status: "running")
   163→
   164→    RouteManager.new.reconnect_routes(sandbox: sandbox) if sandbox.routed?
   165→
   166→    sandbox
   167→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   168→    sandbox.update!(status: "destroyed", container_id: nil)
   169→    raise Error, "Container not found — sandbox must be recreated"
   170→  end
   171→
   172→  def stop(sandbox:)
   173→    return sandbox if sandbox.status == "stopped"
   174→
   175→    begin
   176→      TerminalManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
   177→    rescue TerminalManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError
   178→      # best-effort terminal cleanup
   179→    end
   180→
   181→    RouteManager.new.suspend_routes(sandbox: sandbox) if sandbox.routed?
   182→
   183→    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   184→    container.stop(t: 10)
   185→    sandbox.update!(status: "stopped")
   186→    sandbox
   187→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   188→    sandbox.update!(status: "destroyed", container_id: nil)
   189→    raise Error, "Container not found"
   190→  end
   191→
   192→  def status(sandbox:)
   193→    return { state: "destroyed" } if sandbox.container_id.blank?
   194→
   195→    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   196→    info = container.json
   197→    {
   198→      state: info.dig("State", "Status"),
   199→      running: info.dig("State", "Running"),
   200→      started_at: info.dig("State", "StartedAt"),
   201→      pid: info.dig("State", "Pid")
   202→    }
   203→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   204→    { state: "not_found" }
   205→  end
   206→
   207→  # Create a composite snapshot (Docker image + optional BTRFS layers).
   208→  # Returns a Snapshot ActiveRecord object.
   209→  #
   210→  # layers: array of "container", "home", "data", "workspace"
   211→  #   nil means "all available" based on sandbox config
   212→  def create_snapshot(sandbox:, name:, label: nil, layers: nil, data_subdir: nil)
   213→    raise Error, "Sandbox has no running container" if sandbox.container_id.blank?
   214→
   215→    user = sandbox.user
   216→    name = name.presence || Date.today.iso8601
   217→    requested_layers = layers&.map(&:to_s) || %w[container home data workspace]
   218→
   219→    snap = Snapshot.new(
   220→      user: user,
   221→      name: name,
   222→      label: label,
   223→      source_sandbox: sandbox.name,
   224→      data_subdir: data_subdir
   225→    )
   226→
   227→    # ── Container layer ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   228→    if requested_layers.include?("container")
   229→      repo = "sc-snap-#{user.name}"
   230→      container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   231→      image = container.commit(
   232→        repo: repo,
   233→        tag: name,
   234→        comment: "sandbox:#{sandbox.name}"
   235→      )
   236→      snap.docker_image = "#{repo}:#{name}"
   237→      snap.docker_size  = image.info["Size"]
   238→    end
   239→
   240→    # ── Home layer (BTRFS only) ───────────────────────────────────────────────
   241→    if requested_layers.include?("home") && sandbox.mount_home? && BtrfsHelper.btrfs?
   242→      home_src  = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/home"
   243→      home_dest = "#{DATA_DIR}/snapshots/#{user.name}/#{name}/home"
   244→      if Dir.exist?(home_src)
   245→        BtrfsHelper.snapshot_subvolume(home_src, home_dest)
   246→        snap.home_snapshot = home_dest
   247→        snap.home_size     = BtrfsHelper.subvolume_size(home_dest)
   248→      end
   249→    end
   250→
   251→    # ── Data layer (BTRFS only) ───────────────────────────────────────────────
   252→    if requested_layers.include?("data") && sandbox.data_path.present? && BtrfsHelper.btrfs?
   253→      if data_subdir.present?
   254→        data_src  = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/data/#{sandbox.data_path}/#{data_subdir}".chomp("/")
   255→      else
   256→        data_src  = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/data/#{sandbox.data_path}".chomp("/")
   257→      end
   258→      data_dest = "#{DATA_DIR}/snapshots/#{user.name}/#{name}/data"
   259→      if Dir.exist?(data_src)
   260→        BtrfsHelper.snapshot_subvolume(data_src, data_dest)
   261→        snap.data_snapshot = data_dest
   262→        snap.data_size     = BtrfsHelper.subvolume_size(data_dest)
   263→      end
   264→    end
   265→
   266→    # ── Workspace layer (BTRFS only) ─────────────────────────────────────────
   267→    if requested_layers.include?("workspace") && sandbox.persistent_volume? && sandbox.volume_path.present? && BtrfsHelper.btrfs?
   268→      workspace_src  = sandbox.volume_path
   269→      workspace_dest = "#{DATA_DIR}/snapshots/#{user.name}/#{name}/workspace"
   270→      if Dir.exist?(workspace_src)
   271→        BtrfsHelper.snapshot_subvolume(workspace_src, workspace_dest)
   272→        # Store workspace alongside data_snapshot when no data layer taken
   273→        snap.data_snapshot ||= workspace_dest
   274→        snap.data_size     = BtrfsHelper.subvolume_size(workspace_dest)
   275→      end
   276→    end
   277→
   278→    snap.save!
   279→    snap
   280→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   281→    raise Error, "Failed to create snapshot: #{e.message}"
   282→  rescue BtrfsHelper::Error => e
   283→    raise Error, "Failed to snapshot filesystem layer: #{e.message}"
   284→  end
   285→
   286→  # Legacy alias kept for backward compatibility (used by existing API endpoint).
   287→  def snapshot(sandbox:, name: nil, **opts)
   288→    snap = create_snapshot(sandbox: sandbox, name: name, layers: %w[container])
   289→    {
   290→      name: snap.name,
   291→      image: snap.docker_image,
   292→      sandbox: snap.source_sandbox,
   293→      created_at: snap.created_at
   294→    }
   295→  end
   296→
   297→  def list_snapshots(user:)
   298→    import_legacy_snapshots(user)
   299→    Snapshot.where(user: user).order(created_at: :desc).map { |s| snapshot_json(s) }
   300→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   301→    raise Error, "Failed to list snapshots: #{e.message}"
   302→  end
   303→
   304→  def find_snapshot(user:, name:)
   305→    import_legacy_snapshots(user)
   306→    Snapshot.find_by!(user: user, name: name)
   307→  rescue ActiveRecord::RecordNotFound
   308→    raise Error, "Snapshot '#{name}' not found"
   309→  end
   310→
   311→  def destroy_snapshot(user:, name:)
   312→    snap = Snapshot.find_by(user: user, name: name)
   313→
   314→    if snap
   315→      # Remove Docker image
   316→      if snap.docker_image.present?
   317→        begin
   318→          Docker::Image.get(snap.docker_image).remove
   319→        rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   320→          # Already gone
   321→        rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   322→          raise Error, "Failed to remove Docker image: #{e.message}"
   323→        end
   324→      end
   325→
   326→      # Remove BTRFS snapshots
   327→      if snap.home_snapshot.present?
   328→        begin
   329→          BtrfsHelper.delete_snapshot(snap.home_snapshot)
   330→        rescue BtrfsHelper::Error => e
   331→          Rails.logger.warn("Could not delete home snapshot #{snap.home_snapshot}: #{e.message}")
   332→        end
   333→      end
   334→
   335→      if snap.data_snapshot.present?
   336→        begin
   337→          BtrfsHelper.delete_snapshot(snap.data_snapshot)
   338→        rescue BtrfsHelper::Error => e
   339→          Rails.logger.warn("Could not delete data snapshot #{snap.data_snapshot}: #{e.message}")
   340→        end
   341→      end
   342→
   343→      snap.destroy!
   344→    else
   345→      # Legacy: try to find and remove Docker image directly
   346→      image_ref = "sc-snap-#{user.name}:#{name}"
   347→      begin
   348→        Docker::Image.get(image_ref).remove
   349→      rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   350→        raise Error, "Snapshot '#{name}' not found"
   351→      rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   352→        raise Error, "Failed to destroy snapshot: #{e.message}"
   353→      end
   354→    end
   355→  end
   356→
   357→  def restore(sandbox:, snapshot_name:, layers: nil)
   358→    user = sandbox.user
   359→    was_tailscale = sandbox.tailscale?
   360→
   361→    snap = Snapshot.find_by(user: user, name: snapshot_name)
   362→    requested_layers = layers&.map(&:to_s)
   363→
   364→    # Determine the Docker image to use
   365→    if snap&.docker_image.present?
   366→      image_ref = snap.docker_image
   367→    else
   368→      # Fall back to legacy naming
   369→      image_ref = "sc-snap-#{user.name}:#{snapshot_name}"
   370→    end
   371→
   372→    restore_container = requested_layers.nil? || requested_layers.include?("container")
   373→    restore_home      = snap&.home_snapshot.present? && (requested_layers.nil? || requested_layers.include?("home"))
   374→    restore_data      = snap&.data_snapshot.present? && (requested_layers.nil? || requested_layers.include?("data"))
   375→
   376→    # Validate the Docker image exists if we need it
   377→    if restore_container
   378→      begin
   379→        Docker::Image.get(image_ref)
   380→      rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   381→        raise Error, "Snapshot '#{snapshot_name}' not found"
   382→      end
   383→    end
   384→
   385→    begin
   386→      TerminalManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
   387→    rescue TerminalManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError
   388→      # best-effort terminal cleanup
   389→    end
   390→
   391→    begin
   392→      VncManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
   393→    rescue VncManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError
   394→      # best-effort VNC cleanup
   395→    end
   396→
   397→    if sandbox.tailscale?
   398→      TailscaleManager.new.disconnect_sandbox(sandbox: sandbox)
   399→    end
   400→
   401→    if sandbox.container_id.present?
   402→      begin
   403→        old_container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   404→        old_container.stop(t: 5) rescue nil
   405→        old_container.delete(force: true)
   406→      rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   407→        # Already gone
   408→      end
   409→    end
   410→
   411→    # ── Restore home directory ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   412→    if restore_home
   413→      home_target = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/home"
   414→      begin
   415→        BtrfsHelper.restore_subvolume(snap.home_snapshot, home_target)
   416→      rescue BtrfsHelper::Error => e
   417→        Rails.logger.warn("Could not restore home snapshot: #{e.message}")
   418→      end
   419→    end
   420→
   421→    # ── Restore data directory ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   422→    if restore_data
   423→      if snap.data_subdir.present?
   424→        data_target = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/data/#{sandbox.data_path}/#{snap.data_subdir}".chomp("/")
   425→      else
   426→        data_target = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/data/#{sandbox.data_path}".chomp("/")
   427→      end
   428→      begin
   429→        BtrfsHelper.restore_subvolume(snap.data_snapshot, data_target)
   430→      rescue BtrfsHelper::Error => e
   431→        Rails.logger.warn("Could not restore data snapshot: #{e.message}")
   432→      end
   433→    end
   434→
   435→    # ── Recreate container ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   436→    final_image = restore_container ? image_ref : sandbox.image
   437→
   438→    container = Docker::Container.create(
   439→      "name" => sandbox.full_name,
   440→      "Image" => final_image,
   441→      "Hostname" => sandbox.full_name,
   442→      "Env" => container_env(user, sandbox),
   443→      "HostConfig" => {
   444→        "Runtime" => container_runtime,
   445→        "PortBindings" => {
   446→          "22/tcp" => [ { "HostPort" => sandbox.ssh_port.to_s } ]
   447→        },
   448→        "Binds" => volume_binds(user, sandbox),
   449→        "RestartPolicy" => { "Name" => "unless-stopped" }
   450→      }
   451→    )
   452→
   453→    container.start
   454→    sandbox.update!(container_id: container.id, image: final_image, status: "running")
   455→
   456→    if was_tailscale && user.tailscale_enabled?
   457→      TailscaleManager.new.connect_sandbox(sandbox: sandbox)
   458→    end
   459→
   460→    sandbox
   461→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   462→    raise Error, "Failed to restore snapshot: #{e.message}"
   463→  end
   464→
   465→  # Import legacy Docker-only snapshots as DB records (idempotent).
   466→  def import_legacy_snapshots(user)
   467→    repo_prefix = "sc-snap-#{user.name}"
   468→
   469→    Docker::Image.all.each do |img|
   470→      repo_tags = img.info["RepoTags"] || []
   471→      repo_tags.each do |tag|
   472→        repo, tag_name = tag.split(":")
   473→        next unless repo == repo_prefix
   474→        next if tag_name.blank?
   475→        next if Snapshot.exists?(user: user, name: tag_name)
   476→
   477→        Snapshot.create!(
   478→          user: user,
   479→          name: tag_name,
   480→          docker_image: tag,
   481→          docker_size: img.info["Size"],
   482→          source_sandbox: img.info.dig("Comment")&.delete_prefix("sandbox:"),
   483→          created_at: Time.at(img.info["Created"] || Time.current.to_i)
   484→        )
   485→      end
   486→    end
   487→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError, ActiveRecord::RecordInvalid => e
   488→    Rails.logger.warn("Legacy snapshot import failed: #{e.message}")
   489→  end
   490→
   491→  def snapshot_json(snap)
   492→    {
   493→      name: snap.name,
   494→      label: snap.label,
   495→      source_sandbox: snap.source_sandbox,
   496→      layers: snap.layers,
   497→      docker_image: snap.docker_image,
   498→      docker_size: snap.docker_size,
   499→      home_size: snap.home_size,
   500→      data_size: snap.data_size,
   501→      total_size: snap.total_size,
   502→      created_at: snap.created_at
   503→    }
   504→  end
   505→
   506→  def connect_info(sandbox:)
   507→    host = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_HOST", "localhost")
   508→    user = sandbox.user.name
   509→    info = {
   510→      host: host,
   511→      port: sandbox.ssh_port,
   512→      user: user,
   513→      command: sandbox.connect_command(host: host)
   514→    }
   515→
   516→    # If Tailscale is enabled, wait for and use the bridge network IP for direct SSH access
   517→    # This works if the client is on the same Tailscale network (routes advertised by sidecar)
   518→    if sandbox.tailscale?
   519→      ts_ip = wait_for_tailscale_ip(sandbox: sandbox)
   520→      if ts_ip.present?
   521→        info[:host] = ts_ip
   522→        info[:port] = 22
   523→        info[:command] = "ssh #{user}@#{ts_ip}"
   524→        info[:tailscale_ip] = ts_ip
   525→      end
   526→    end
   527→
   528→    info
   529→  end
   530→
   531→  private
   532→
   533→  def wait_for_tailscale_ip(sandbox:, max_attempts: 30, delay: 0.5)
   534→    # Wait for the sandbox to be provisioned (background job) and Tailscale IP to be assigned
   535→    max_attempts.times do
   536→      sandbox.reload # Refresh from DB to get latest status
   537→
   538→      # If sandbox isn't running yet, keep waiting (provision job in progress)
   539→      if sandbox.status != "running"
   540→        sleep delay
   541→        next
   542→      end
   543→
   544→      # Sandbox is running, try to get Tailscale IP
   545→      ts_ip = TailscaleManager.new.sandbox_tailscale_ip(sandbox: sandbox)
   546→      return ts_ip if ts_ip.present?
   547→
   548→      sleep delay
   549→    end
   550→    Rails.logger.warn("Tailscale IP not available for sandbox #{sandbox.id} after #{max_attempts} attempts (status: #{sandbox.status})")
   551→    nil
   552→  end
   553→
   554→  def container_env(user, sandbox)
   555→    env = [
   556→      "SANDCASTLE_USER=#{user.name}",
   557→      "SANDCASTLE_SSH_KEY=#{user.ssh_public_key}"
   558→    ]
   559→    env << "USER_EMAIL=#{user.email_address}" if user.email_address.present?
   560→    env << "USER_FULLNAME=#{user.full_name}" if user.full_name.present?
   561→    env << "SANDCASTLE_VNC_ENABLED=#{sandbox.vnc_enabled? ? '1' : '0'}"
   562→    env << "SANDCASTLE_VNC_GEOMETRY=#{sandbox.vnc_geometry}"
   563→    env << "SANDCASTLE_VNC_DEPTH=#{sandbox.vnc_depth}"
   564→    env
   565→  end
   566→
   567→  def container_runtime
   568→    @container_runtime ||= begin
   569→      runtimes = Docker.info["Runtimes"] || {}
   570→      if runtimes.key?("sysbox-runc")
   571→        "sysbox-runc"
   572→      else
   573→        Rails.logger.warn("SandboxManager: sysbox-runc not available, falling back to runc (Docker-in-Docker will not work inside sandboxes)")
   574→        "runc"
   575→      end
   576→    end
   577→  end
   578→
   579→  def volume_binds(user, sandbox)
   580→    binds = []
   581→    if sandbox.mount_home
   582→      binds << "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/home:/home/#{user.name}"
   583→    end
   584→    if sandbox.persistent_volume && sandbox.volume_path
   585→      binds << "#{sandbox.volume_path}:/workspace"
   586→    end
   587→    if sandbox.data_path.present?
   588→      host_path = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/data/#{sandbox.data_path}".chomp("/")
   589→      binds << "#{host_path}:/data"
   590→    end
   591→    # Chrome profile persistence: mount separately if not mounting full home
   592→    if user.chrome_persist_profile? && !sandbox.mount_home
   593→      host_path = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/chrome-profile"
   594→      binds << "#{host_path}:/home/#{user.name}/.config/google-chrome"
   595→    end
   596→    binds
   597→  end
   598→end
   599→
```

> AGENT

Port 2201 is claimed by an orphaned container `thies-bright-tiger` that's running but may not be in the DB. Two issues: the error message doesn't tell you *why* the container failed to start, and port conflicts aren't surfaced properly.

Let me fix the error message to include Docker's actual state error:

> TOOL

```json
  "new_string": "    container.start\n    container.refresh!\n    unless container.json.dig(\"State\", \"Running\")\n      state_error = container.json.dig(\"State\", \"Error\").presence || container.json.dig(\"State\", \"Status\")\n      raise Error, \"Container failed to start: #{state_error}\"\n    end"
}
```

> AGENT

The current `assign_ssh_port` only checks the DB — orphaned Docker containers holding ports aren't considered. I'll add a Docker port check to `SandboxManager` and pre-assign the port before validation fires:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_019oz7hDtMMJL9PaN14ZYSxY
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "    # Build sandbox record (not saved yet)\n    sandbox = user.sandboxes.build(\n      name: name,\n      image: image,\n      status: \"pending\",\n      persistent_volume: persistent,\n      mount_home: mount_home,\n      data_path: data_path,\n      temporary: temporary\n    )\n\n    if persistent\n      sandbox.volume_path = \"#{DATA_DIR}/sandboxes/#{sandbox.full_name}/vol\"\n    end\n\n    # Validate before doing expensive operations\n    sandbox.validate!",
  "new_string": "    # Build sandbox record (not saved yet)\n    sandbox = user.sandboxes.build(\n      name: name,\n      image: image,\n      status: \"pending\",\n      persistent_volume: persistent,\n      mount_home: mount_home,\n      data_path: data_path,\n      temporary: temporary\n    )\n\n    if persistent\n      sandbox.volume_path = \"#{DATA_DIR}/sandboxes/#{sandbox.full_name}/vol\"\n    end\n\n    # Pre-assign a port that is free in both the DB and Docker (skips model callback)\n    sandbox.ssh_port = find_free_ssh_port\n\n    # Validate before doing expensive operations\n    sandbox.validate!"
}
```

Now add the two private helper methods:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01Abx2zChkejXgx7XP3qLb5V
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "  def container_env(user, sandbox)",
  "new_string": "  def find_free_ssh_port\n    db_used = Sandbox.where.not(status: \"destroyed\").pluck(:ssh_port)\n    docker_used = docker_bound_ssh_ports\n    used = (db_used + docker_used).uniq\n    available = Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE.to_a - used\n    available.first || raise(Error, \"No SSH ports available in range #{Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE}\")\n  end\n\n  def docker_bound_ssh_ports\n    Docker::Container.all(all: true).flat_map do |c|\n      (c.info[\"Ports\"] || []).map { |p| p[\"PublicPort\"] }.compact\n    end.select { |p| Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE.include?(p) }\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError\n    []\n  end\n\n  def container_env(user, sandbox)"
}
```

> AGENT

Now addressing the second request — port should be freed (set nil) when stopped, and a fresh one assigned on restart (which requires recreating the container since Docker port bindings are immutable):

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_014UqKMG4u5jNEZDMKr43NSN
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "  def start(sandbox:)\n    raise Error, \"Sandbox is destroyed\" if sandbox.status == \"destroyed\"\n    return sandbox if sandbox.status == \"running\"\n\n    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)\n    container.start\n    sandbox.update!(status: \"running\")\n\n    RouteManager.new.reconnect_routes(sandbox: sandbox) if sandbox.routed?\n\n    sandbox\n  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError\n    sandbox.update!(status: \"destroyed\", container_id: nil)\n    raise Error, \"Container not found — sandbox must be recreated\"\n  end\n\n  def stop(sandbox:)\n    return sandbox if sandbox.status == \"stopped\"\n\n    begin\n      TerminalManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)\n    rescue TerminalManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError\n      # best-effort terminal cleanup\n    end\n\n    RouteManager.new.suspend_routes(sandbox: sandbox) if sandbox.routed?\n\n    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)\n    container.stop(t: 10)\n    sandbox.update!(status: \"stopped\")\n    sandbox\n  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError\n    sandbox.update!(status: \"destroyed\", container_id: nil)\n    raise Error, \"Container not found\"\n  end",
  "new_string": "  def start(sandbox:)\n    raise Error, \"Sandbox is destroyed\" if sandbox.status == \"destroyed\"\n    return sandbox if sandbox.status == \"running\"\n\n    user = sandbox.user\n\n    # Port was released on stop — assign a new free port and recreate the container\n    new_port = find_free_ssh_port\n    sandbox.update!(ssh_port: new_port)\n\n    if sandbox.container_id.present?\n      begin\n        old = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)\n        old.stop(t: 5) rescue nil\n        old.delete(force: true)\n      rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError\n        # already gone\n      end\n    end\n\n    create_container_and_start(sandbox: sandbox, user: user)\n\n    RouteManager.new.reconnect_routes(sandbox: sandbox) if sandbox.routed?\n\n    sandbox\n  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError\n    sandbox.update!(status: \"destroyed\", container_id: nil)\n    raise Error, \"Container not found — sandbox must be recreated\"\n  end\n\n  def stop(sandbox:)\n    return sandbox if sandbox.status == \"stopped\"\n\n    begin\n      TerminalManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)\n    rescue TerminalManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError\n      # best-effort terminal cleanup\n    end\n\n    RouteManager.new.suspend_routes(sandbox: sandbox) if sandbox.routed?\n\n    if sandbox.container_id.present?\n      begin\n        container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)\n        container.stop(t: 10)\n      rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError\n        # already gone\n      end\n    end\n\n    sandbox.update!(status: \"stopped\", ssh_port: nil)\n    sandbox\n  end"
}
```

> AGENT

Also add a TCP connect check to verify the port is truly free at the network level — catches anything the Docker API might miss:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_0145ekcKAGhKkdvPAFNAz1NB
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "  def find_free_ssh_port\n    db_used = Sandbox.where.not(status: %w[destroyed stopped]).pluck(:ssh_port).compact\n    docker_used = docker_bound_ssh_ports\n    used = (db_used + docker_used).uniq\n    available = Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE.to_a - used\n    available.first || raise(Error, \"No SSH ports available in range #{Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE}\")\n  end\n\n  def docker_bound_ssh_ports\n    Docker::Container.all(all: true).flat_map do |c|\n      (c.info[\"Ports\"] || []).map { |p| p[\"PublicPort\"] }.compact\n    end.select { |p| Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE.include?(p) }\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError\n    []\n  end",
  "new_string": "  def find_free_ssh_port\n    db_used = Sandbox.where.not(status: %w[destroyed stopped]).pluck(:ssh_port).compact\n    docker_used = docker_bound_ssh_ports\n    candidates = Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE.to_a - (db_used + docker_used).uniq\n    port = candidates.find { |p| port_free?(p) }\n    port || raise(Error, \"No SSH ports available in range #{Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE}\")\n  end\n\n  def docker_bound_ssh_ports\n    Docker::Container.all(all: true).flat_map do |c|\n      (c.info[\"Ports\"] || []).map { |p| p[\"PublicPort\"] }.compact\n    end.select { |p| Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE.include?(p) }\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError\n    []\n  end\n\n  # Verify a port is truly free by attempting a TCP connection to the Docker host.\n  # Returns true (free) if the connection is refused. Falls back to true if the\n  # Docker host is not reachable (e.g. production Linux without host.docker.internal).\n  def port_free?(port)\n    Timeout.timeout(0.3) { TCPSocket.new(\"host.docker.internal\", port).close }\n    false # connected → port is occupied\n  rescue Errno::ECONNREFUSED, Timeout::Error\n    true  # refused/timed-out → port is free\n  rescue SocketError, Errno::EHOSTUNREACH, Errno::ENETUNREACH\n    true  # host not reachable → skip TCP check, rely on Docker API result\n  end"
}
```

> AGENT

The `RouteManager` overwrites `rails.yml` with cert path `/data/certs/cert.pem`, but we mounted `.cert` at `/certs` — wrong path. The fix: mount `.cert` at `/data/certs` to match what `RouteManager` expects, and remove the redundant TLS config from `init-traefik` (RouteManager owns `rails.yml`):

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01T469Kmjqj9Z7nbbJxi86LS
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/docker-compose.dev.yml",
  "old_string": "    volumes:\n      - traefik-data:/data\n      - ./.cert:/certs:ro",
  "new_string": "    volumes:\n      - traefik-data:/data\n      - ./.cert:/data/certs:ro"
}
```

```
     3→
     4→  def perform
     5→    Sandbox.active.where.not(container_id: nil).find_each do |sandbox|
     6→      sync_sandbox(sandbox)
     7→    end
     8→
     9→    User.where(tailscale_state: [ "enabled", "pending" ]).find_each do |user|
    10→      sync_tailscale_sidecar(user)
    11→    end
    12→
    13→    begin
    14→      RouteManager.new.sync_all_configs
    15→    rescue => e
    16→      Rails.logger.error("ContainerSyncJob: route sync failed: #{e.message}")
    17→    end
    18→
    19→    begin
    20→      TerminalManager.new.cleanup_orphaned
    21→    rescue => e
    22→      Rails.logger.error("ContainerSyncJob: terminal cleanup failed: #{e.message}")
    23→    end
    24→
    25→    begin
    26→      VncManager.new.cleanup_orphaned
    27→    rescue => e
    28→      Rails.logger.error("ContainerSyncJob: VNC cleanup failed: #{e.message}")
    29→    end
    30→  end
    31→
    32→  private
    33→
    34→  def sync_sandbox(sandbox)
    35→    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
    36→    state = container.json["State"] || {}
    37→    actual_status = if state["Restarting"]
    38→      "stopped"
    39→    elsif state["Running"]
    40→      "running"
    41→    else
    42→      "stopped"
    43→    end
    44→
    45→    if sandbox.status != actual_status
    46→      if actual_status == "stopped"
    47→        begin
    48→          TerminalManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
    49→        rescue TerminalManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError
    50→          # best-effort
    51→        end
    52→        begin
    53→          VncManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
    54→        rescue VncManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError
    55→          # best-effort
    56→        end
    57→      end
    58→      sandbox.update!(status: actual_status)
    59→      Rails.logger.info("ContainerSyncJob: #{sandbox.full_name} status corrected to #{actual_status}")
    60→    end
    61→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
    62→    begin
    63→      TerminalManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
    64→    rescue TerminalManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError
    65→      # best-effort
    66→    end
    67→    begin
    68→      VncManager.new.close(sandbox: sandbox)
    69→    rescue VncManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError
    70→      # best-effort
    71→    end
    72→    sandbox.update!(status: "destroyed", container_id: nil)
    73→    Rails.logger.warn("ContainerSyncJob: #{sandbox.full_name} container gone, marked destroyed")
    74→  end
    75→
    76→  def sync_tailscale_sidecar(user)
    77→    return if user.tailscale_container_id.blank?
    78→
    79→    Docker::Container.get(user.tailscale_container_id)
    80→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
    81→    user.update!(tailscale_state: "disabled", tailscale_container_id: nil, tailscale_network: nil)
    82→    user.sandboxes.active.where(tailscale: true).update_all(tailscale: false)
    83→    Rails.logger.warn("ContainerSyncJob: Tailscale sidecar for #{user.name} gone, marked disabled")
    84→  end
    85→end
    86→
```

> AGENT

The issue is a race: `init-traefik` writes `rails.yml` (no certs) and then the Rails initializer overwrites it with the proper cert config on startup. When we force-recreated `init-traefik` + `traefik` without restarting `web`, init-traefik overwrote the good config.

Two fixes: 1) make `web` depend on `init-traefik` so the order is always correct, 2) restart `web` now to trigger the Rails initializer:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_013wsZv2VKvPJJpLTEAehDT7
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/docker-compose.dev.yml",
  "old_string": "    depends_on:\n      init-data:\n        condition: service_completed_successfully\n      migrate:\n        condition: service_completed_successfully\n\n  worker:",
  "new_string": "    depends_on:\n      init-data:\n        condition: service_completed_successfully\n      init-traefik:\n        condition: service_completed_successfully\n      migrate:\n        condition: service_completed_successfully\n\n  worker:"
}
```

> AGENT

`rails.yml` now has the cert config pointing to `/data/certs/cert.pem`, which maps to `.cert/cert.pem` via the corrected mount. Traefik should pick it up via file-watch. The page should now show the mkcert cert again.

> DEVELOPER

will this break letsencrypt in prod?

> AGENT

No — the cert injection in `RouteManager#write_rails_config` is already guarded by `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE`:

```ruby
if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
  config["tls"] = { "certificates" => certs }
end
```

In production with Let's Encrypt, `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE` is not `selfsigned`, so no `tls.certificates` block is ever written to `rails.yml`. Traefik's ACME resolver handles the cert entirely separately.

The only file we changed that touches production is `sandbox_manager.rb` (the port logic), `sandbox.rb` (the validation), and the shared views — none of which touch TLS. The `docker-compose.dev.yml` mount (`.cert:/data/certs:ro`) only exists in the dev compose file.

> AGENT

The problem: Tailwind v4 doesn't detect `pt-28` inside a Ruby string interpolation. Fix with `@source inline()` to force-include dynamic utility classes:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01NjU2STgZseGMQWKP1cDcNw
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/assets/tailwind/application.css",
  "old_string": "@import \"tailwindcss\";",
  "new_string": "@import \"tailwindcss\";\n\n/* Force-include dynamic utility classes used in Ruby string interpolations\n   that Tailwind's scanner cannot detect at build time. */\n@source inline(\"pt-28 pt-40 mt-36\");"
}
```

> AGENT

There it is — `if Rails.env.production?`. The initializer only runs in production, but the dev container runs in `development` env. The cert config never gets written in dev.

Fix: remove the environment guard so it runs in all envs:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01KEQUzr9jqRmgRa38ZMKEWs
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/config/initializers/traefik.rb",
  "old_string": "Rails.application.config.after_initialize do\n  if Rails.env.production?\n    host = ENV[\"SANDCASTLE_HOST\"]\n    if host.present?\n      begin\n        RouteManager.new.write_rails_config(host: host)\n        Rails.logger.info(\"Traefik: wrote Rails route config for #{host}\")\n      rescue => e\n        Rails.logger.warn(\"Traefik: failed to write Rails route config: #{e.message}\")\n      end\n    end\n  end\nend",
  "new_string": "Rails.application.config.after_initialize do\n  host = ENV[\"SANDCASTLE_HOST\"]\n  if host.present?\n    begin\n      RouteManager.new.write_rails_config(host: host)\n      Rails.logger.info(\"Traefik: wrote Rails route config for #{host}\")\n    rescue => e\n      Rails.logger.warn(\"Traefik: failed to write Rails route config: #{e.message}\")\n    end\n  end\nend"
}
```

```
     8→  def add_route(sandbox:, domain:, port: 8080)
     9→    raise Error, "Sandbox is not running" unless sandbox.status == "running"
    10→
    11→    route = sandbox.routes.create!(domain: domain, port: port)
    12→
    13→    ensure_network

    15→    write_config(sandbox)

    17→    route

    19→    raise Error, e.message
    20→  end
    21→
    22→  def remove_route(route:)
    23→    sandbox = route.sandbox
    24→    route.destroy!
    25→
    26→    if sandbox.routes.reload.any?
    27→      write_config(sandbox)
    28→    else
    29→      delete_config(sandbox)
    30→      disconnect_from_network(sandbox)
    31→    end
    32→  end
    33→
    34→  def remove_all_routes(sandbox:)
    35→    return unless sandbox.routed?
    36→
    37→    sandbox.routes.destroy_all
    38→    delete_config(sandbox)
    39→    disconnect_from_network(sandbox)
    40→  end
    41→
    42→  def suspend_routes(sandbox:)
    43→    return unless sandbox.routed?
    44→
    45→    delete_config(sandbox)
    46→    disconnect_from_network(sandbox)

    49→  def reconnect_routes(sandbox:)
    50→    return unless sandbox.routed?
    51→
    52→    ensure_network
    53→    connect_to_network(sandbox)
    54→    write_config(sandbox)
    55→  end
    56→
    57→  def sync_all_configs
    58→    FileUtils.mkdir_p(DYNAMIC_DIR)
    59→
    60→    active_ids = Sandbox.active.running.joins(:routes).distinct.pluck(:id).to_set
    61→
    62→    # Remove stale config files
    63→    Dir.glob(File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "sandbox-*.yml")).each do |path|
    64→      id = File.basename(path, ".yml").delete_prefix("sandbox-").to_i
    65→      unless active_ids.include?(id)
    66→        File.delete(path)
    67→        Rails.logger.info("RouteManager: removed stale config #{File.basename(path)}")
    68→      end
    69→    end
    70→
    71→    # Regenerate configs for active routed sandboxes
    72→    Sandbox.active.running.joins(:routes).distinct.includes(:user, :routes).find_each do |sandbox|
    73→      ensure_network
    74→      connect_to_network(sandbox)
    75→      write_config(sandbox)
    76→    end
    77→  end
    78→
    79→  def write_rails_config(host:)
    80→    FileUtils.mkdir_p(DYNAMIC_DIR)
    81→
    82→    # Build host list: main host + optional alternative hostnames
    83→    hosts = [ host ]
    84→    alt_hostnames = ENV["SANDCASTLE_ALT_HOSTNAMES"].to_s.split(",").map(&:strip).reject(&:empty?)
    85→    hosts += alt_hostnames
    86→
    87→    rule = if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
    88→      "HostRegexp(`.+`)"
    89→    else
    90→      host_rules = hosts.map { |h| "`#{h}`" }.join(", ")
    91→      "Host(#{host_rules})"
    92→    end
    93→
    94→    config = {
    95→      "http" => {
    96→        "routers" => {
    97→          "rails-http" => {
    98→            "rule" => rule,
    99→            "service" => "rails",
   100→            "entryPoints" => [ "web" ]
   101→          },
   102→          "rails-https" => {
   103→            "rule" => rule,
   104→            "service" => "rails",
   105→            "entryPoints" => [ "websecure" ],
   106→            "tls" => tls_config
   107→          }
   108→        },
   109→        "services" => {
   110→          "rails" => {
   111→            "loadBalancer" => {
   112→              "servers" => [ { "url" => "http://sandcastle-web:80" } ]
   113→            }
   114→          }
   115→        }
   116→      }
   117→    }
   118→
   119→    # Add TLS certificates section for self-signed mode
   120→    if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
   121→      certs = [
   122→        {
   123→          "certFile" => "/data/certs/cert.pem",
   124→          "keyFile" => "/data/certs/key.pem"
   125→        }
   126→      ]
   127→
   128→      # Add custom certificates if configured
   129→      if custom_cert_configured?
   130→        certs << {
   131→          "certFile" => "/data/certs/custom-cert.pem",
   132→          "keyFile" => "/data/certs/custom-key.pem"
   133→        }
   134→      end
   135→
   136→      config["tls"] = { "certificates" => certs }
   137→    end
   138→
   139→    File.write(File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "rails.yml"), config.to_yaml)
   140→  end
   141→
   142→  private
   143→
   144→  def custom_cert_configured?
   145→    cert_path = File.join(DATA_DIR, "traefik", "certs", "custom-cert.pem")
   146→    key_path = File.join(DATA_DIR, "traefik", "certs", "custom-key.pem")
   147→    File.exist?(cert_path) && File.exist?(key_path)
   148→  end
   149→
   150→  def config_path(sandbox)
   151→    File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "sandbox-#{sandbox.id}.yml")
   152→  end
   153→
   154→  def write_config(sandbox)
   155→    FileUtils.mkdir_p(DYNAMIC_DIR)
   156→
   157→    routes = sandbox.routes.reload
   158→    routers = {}
   159→    services = {}
   160→
   161→    routes.each do |route|
   162→      key = "sandbox-#{sandbox.id}-r#{route.id}"
   163→      routers[key] = {
   164→        "rule" => "Host(`#{route.domain}`)",
   165→        "service" => key,
   166→        "entryPoints" => [ "websecure" ],
   167→        "tls" => tls_config
   168→      }
   169→      services[key] = {
   170→        "loadBalancer" => {
   171→          "servers" => [ { "url" => "http://#{sandbox.full_name}:#{route.port}" } ]
   172→        }
   173→      }
   174→    end
   175→
   176→    config = { "http" => { "routers" => routers, "services" => services } }
   177→    File.write(config_path(sandbox), config.to_yaml)
   178→  end
   179→
   180→  def tls_config
   181→    if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
   182→      {}
   183→    else
   184→      { "certResolver" => "letsencrypt" }
   185→    end
   186→  end
   187→
   188→  def delete_config(sandbox)
   189→    path = config_path(sandbox)
   190→    File.delete(path) if File.exist?(path)
   191→  end
   192→
   193→  def ensure_network
   194→    Docker::Network.get(NETWORK_NAME)
   195→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   196→    Docker::Network.create(NETWORK_NAME, "Driver" => "bridge")
   197→  end
   198→
   199→  def connect_to_network(sandbox)
   200→    return unless sandbox.container_id.present?
   201→
   202→    network = Docker::Network.get(NETWORK_NAME)
   203→    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   204→
   205→    # Check if already connected
   206→    networks = container.json.dig("NetworkSettings", "Networks") || {}
   207→    return if networks.key?(NETWORK_NAME)
   208→
   209→    network.connect(sandbox.container_id)
   210→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   211→    raise Error, "Failed to connect container to network: #{e.message}"
   212→  end
   213→
   214→  def disconnect_from_network(sandbox)
   215→    return unless sandbox.container_id.present?
   216→
   217→    network = Docker::Network.get(NETWORK_NAME)
   218→    network.disconnect(sandbox.container_id)
   219→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   220→    # Network or container already gone
   221→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError
   222→    # Container may not be connected
   223→  end
   224→end
   225→
```

> AGENT

The real problem is architectural: both `init-traefik` and the Rails initializer write to the **same** `rails.yml`. Whenever `init-traefik` re-runs (any `docker compose up` that touches traefik), it overwrites the cert config. The cert should live in its **own file** that `init-traefik` never touches.

Fix: split the TLS cert config into a dedicated `tls.yml`, written only by Rails:

> TOOL

```json
}
```

> AGENT

Now I have the full picture. The fix is clear: extract the `tls.certificates` block into a separate `tls.yml` file that `init-traefik` never writes.

```
[main 5634750] fix(traefik): separate TLS cert config into tls.yml to prevent override
 1 file changed, 14 insertions(+), 17 deletions(-)
```

> AGENT

Done. Here's why this is the permanent fix:

**Root cause:** `rails.yml` was being written by both:
1. `init-traefik` (busybox) — HTTP router config, **no certs**
2. `RouteManager#write_rails_config` — HTTP router config + TLS certs

Every time `docker compose up` triggered `init-traefik`, it overwrote `rails.yml` and wiped the cert config.

**The fix:** TLS cert config now lives in `tls.yml` — a file `init-traefik` never touches. Traefik's file provider loads all `.yml` files from the dynamic directory, so `tls.yml` is picked up automatically. `rails.yml` only contains HTTP router/service config, matching what `init-traefik` writes, so they no longer conflict.

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

The bind mount is NOT working — Docker Desktop on macOS won't overlay a bind mount on a subdirectory of a named volume. The `.cert` directory in the `traefik-data` volume (created by `init-traefik`) shadows the bind mount completely.

Fix: mount the certs at a path outside the named volume, and make the cert dir configurable in RouteManager.

> TOOL

Two changes needed:
1. Mount `.cert` at `/certs` (outside the named volume) in Traefik
2. Remove `mkdir -p /data/certs` from `init-traefik` (not needed anymore)
3. Add `SANDCASTLE_TLS_CERT_DIR=/certs` to the web/worker env, and use it in RouteManager

> AGENT

The `HostRegexp(`.+`)` was intended as a wildcard but Traefik v3 can't extract a domain from it for TLS. In selfsigned mode the cert is for a specific host, so use the same `Host(...)` rule as production:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01Fa2NtBMEwHPzC7889opjRx
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/route_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "    rule = if ENV[\"SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE\"] == \"selfsigned\"\n      \"HostRegexp(`.+`)\"\n    else\n      host_rules = hosts.map { |h| \"`#{h}`\" }.join(\", \")\n      \"Host(#{host_rules})\"\n    end",
  "new_string": "    host_rules = hosts.map { |h| \"`#{h}`\" }.join(\", \")\n    rule = \"Host(#{host_rules})\""
}
```


> AGENT

Clean — no errors or warnings in the last 2 minutes. 

Here's a summary of what was fixed and why it's permanent now:

**Root cause 1: Bind mount shadowing failure**
Docker Desktop on macOS won't overlay a bind mount (`./cert:/data/certs`) on top of a subdirectory of a named volume (`traefik-data:/data`). The volume's empty `/data/certs/` directory wins, hiding your actual cert files.

**Fix:** Mount certs at `/certs` (outside the named volume) via `SANDCASTLE_TLS_CERT_DIR=/certs`. `RouteManager` now uses this env var to write the correct path in `tls.yml`.

**Root cause 2: `tls.yml` was in `rails.yml`**
`init-traefik` overwrote `rails.yml` on every `docker compose up`, wiping the cert config.

**Fix (from previous commit):** TLS cert config now lives in `tls.yml` which `init-traefik` never touches.

**Root cause 3: `HostRegexp(`.+`)` Traefik v3 warning**
Traefik v3 can't extract a domain from a pure regex rule for SNI-based TLS routing. The rule is now `Host(...)` for both selfsigned and letsencrypt modes.

~/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle [main] % curl -v http://sandcastle.tc:8443/
* Host sandcastle.tc:8443 was resolved.
* IPv6: ::1
* IPv4: 127.0.0.1
*   Trying [::1]:8443...
* Connected to sandcastle.tc (::1) port 8443
> GET / HTTP/1.1
> Host: sandcastle.tc:8443
> User-Agent: curl/8.7.1
> Accept: */*
>
* Request completely sent off
< HTTP/1.1 404 Not Found
< Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8
< X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff
< Date: Thu, 19 Feb 2026 19:21:54 GMT
< Content-Length: 19
<
404 page not found
* Connection #0 to host sandcastle.tc left intact

> AGENT

You're hitting the HTTPS port with plain HTTP. The `8443` port is Traefik's `websecure` (TLS) entrypoint — use `https://`:

```
curl -k https://sandcastle.tc:8443/
```

The `-k` skips cert verification (or omit it if you've trusted the mkcert CA in your system keychain). The `404 page not found` you got is Traefik's response to a non-TLS request arriving on the TLS entrypoint — it doesn't match any router and Traefik returns its default 404.

HTTP (port 8080) → `rails-http` router → Rails  
HTTPS (port 8443) → `rails-https` router → Rails (with TLS termination)

> AGENT

The 404 from Traefik means no router matched. Earlier I noticed `"Traefik: wrote Rails route config for sandcastle.local"` — if your shell has `SANDCASTLE_HOST=sandcastle.local` set, Rails overwrites the router rule to `Host('sandcastle.local')` instead of `sandcastle.tc`. Let me check:

> TOOL

id: toolu_013tNYFwYeBwp9XAejcXB3V1

```json
  "command": "docker exec sandcastle-web cat /data/traefik/dynamic/rails.yml && echo \"---ENV---\" && docker exec sandcastle-web env | grep SANDCASTLE",
  "description": "Check rails.yml content and env vars"
}
```

```
      tls: {}
```

> AGENT

Your shell has `SANDCASTLE_HOST=sandcastle.local` and `SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL=https://localhost:8443` set, which override the compose file defaults. Also `SANDCASTLE_TLS_CERT_DIR` is missing entirely (the force-recreate after the PID issue reverted to a plain restart). Fix: hardcode the dev values so shell env can't override them:

> AGENT

Now try `https://sandcastle.tc:8443/` in the browser — it should hit Rails. If the padlock still shows untrusted, you'll need to trust the mkcert CA:

```bash
mkcert -install
```

That adds the mkcert root CA to your system keychain so Safari/Chrome trusts it automatically.

```
class SandboxesController < ApplicationController
  before_action :set_sandbox, only: [ :show, :destroy, :start, :stop, :retry ]

  def new
    authorize Sandbox
    @snapshots = SandboxManager.new.list_snapshots(user: Current.user)
    @btrfs_available = BtrfsHelper.btrfs?
  end

  def show
    @sandbox_snapshots = SandboxManager.new.list_snapshots(user: Current.user)
                           .select { |s| s[:source_sandbox] == @sandbox.name }
    @btrfs = BtrfsHelper.btrfs?
  end

  def create
    authorize Sandbox

    from_snapshot_name = params[:snapshot].presence

    image = if from_snapshot_name.present?
      snap = Snapshot.find_by(user: Current.user, name: from_snapshot_name)
      snap&.docker_image || "sc-snap-#{Current.user.name}:#{from_snapshot_name}"
    else
      params[:image].presence || SandboxManager::DEFAULT_IMAGE
    end

    # Build sandbox record
    # Note: temporary sandboxes can only be created via CLI
    sandbox = Current.user.sandboxes.build(
      name: params.require(:name),
      status: "pending",
      image: image,
      persistent_volume: params[:persistent] == "1",
      mount_home: params[:mount_home] == "1",
      data_path: params[:data_path].presence,
      tailscale: params[:tailscale] == "1",
      vnc_enabled: params[:vnc_enabled] != "0",
      vnc_geometry: Sandbox::VNC_GEOMETRIES.include?(params[:vnc_geometry]) ? params[:vnc_geometry] : "1280x900",
      vnc_depth: Sandbox::VNC_DEPTHS.include?(params[:vnc_depth].to_i) ? params[:vnc_depth].to_i : 24,
      temporary: false
    )

    if sandbox.persistent_volume
      sandbox.volume_path = "#{SandboxManager::DATA_DIR}/sandboxes/#{sandbox.full_name}/vol"
    end

    if sandbox.save
      # Enqueue async job
      SandboxProvisionJob.perform_later(sandbox_id: sandbox.id)

      # Optimistic redirect - dashboard will update via Turbo
      redirect_to root_path, notice: "Creating sandcastle #{sandbox.name}..."
    else
      @snapshots = SandboxManager.new.list_snapshots(user: Current.user)
      flash.now[:alert] = "Failed to create sandbox: #{sandbox.errors.full_messages.join(', ')}"
      render :new, status: :unprocessable_entity
    end
  rescue => e
    @snapshots = SandboxManager.new.list_snapshots(user: Current.user)
    flash.now[:alert] = "Failed to create sandbox: #{e.message}"
    render :new, status: :unprocessable_entity
  end

  def destroy
    if @sandbox.job_in_progress?
      redirect_to root_path, alert: "Operation already in progress"
      return
    end

    @sandbox.start_job("destroying")
    SandboxDestroyJob.perform_later(sandbox_id: @sandbox.id)

    respond_to do |format|
      format.html { redirect_to root_path, notice: "Destroying sandcastle #{@sandbox.name}..." }
      format.turbo_stream { render turbo_stream: turbo_stream.replace(@sandbox, partial: "dashboard/sandbox", locals: { sandbox: @sandbox }) }
    end
  end

  def start
    if @sandbox.job_in_progress?
      redirect_to root_path, alert: "Operation already in progress"
      return
    end

    @sandbox.start_job("starting")
    SandboxStartJob.perform_later(sandbox_id: @sandbox.id)

    respond_to do |format|
      format.html { redirect_to root_path, notice: "Starting sandcastle #{@sandbox.name}..." }
      format.turbo_stream { render turbo_stream: turbo_stream.replace(@sandbox, partial: "dashboard/sandbox", locals: { sandbox: @sandbox }) }
    end
  end

  def stop
    if @sandbox.job_in_progress?
      redirect_to root_path, alert: "Operation already in progress"
      return
    end

    @sandbox.start_job("stopping")
    SandboxStopJob.perform_later(sandbox_id: @sandbox.id)

    respond_to do |format|
      format.html { redirect_to root_path, notice: "Stopping sandcastle #{@sandbox.name}..." }
      format.turbo_stream { render turbo_stream: turbo_stream.replace(@sandbox, partial: "dashboard/sandbox", locals: { sandbox: @sandbox }) }
    end
  end

  def retry
    return unless @sandbox.job_failed?

    @sandbox.update!(job_error: nil)

    case @sandbox.status
    when "destroyed", "pending"
      redirect_to root_path, alert: "Cannot retry creation. Please create a new sandbox."
    when "stopped"
      SandboxStartJob.perform_later(sandbox_id: @sandbox.id)
      redirect_to root_path, notice: "Retrying start..."
    when "running"
      redirect_to root_path, alert: "Sandbox is already running"
    end
  end

  private

  def set_sandbox
    @sandbox = policy_scope(Sandbox).find(params[:id])
    authorize @sandbox
  end
end
```

```
class SandboxProvisionJob < ApplicationJob
  queue_as :default

  def perform(sandbox_id:)
    sandbox = Sandbox.find(sandbox_id)
    return if sandbox.status == "running" # Idempotent

    sandbox.start_job("creating")
    manager = SandboxManager.new

    begin
      # Directory creation
      manager.ensure_mount_dirs(sandbox.user, sandbox)

      # Image pull (can be slow)
      manager.ensure_image(sandbox.image)

      # Container creation and start
      manager.create_container_and_start(sandbox: sandbox, user: sandbox.user)

      # Connect to Tailscale if enabled
      if sandbox.tailscale? && sandbox.user.tailscale_enabled?
        TailscaleManager.new.connect_sandbox(sandbox: sandbox)
      end

      sandbox.update!(status: "running")
      sandbox.finish_job

    rescue => e
      Rails.logger.error("SandboxProvisionJob failed: #{e.message}\n#{e.backtrace.join("\n")}")
      sandbox.fail_job("Failed to create: #{e.message}")
      sandbox.update!(status: "destroyed")

      raise # Re-raise for Solid Queue retry
    end
  end
end
```

```
   107→
```

```
class SandboxStartJob < ApplicationJob
  queue_as :default

  def perform(sandbox_id:)
    sandbox = Sandbox.find(sandbox_id)
    return if sandbox.status == "running" # Idempotent

    begin
      SandboxManager.new.start(sandbox: sandbox)
      sandbox.finish_job
    rescue => e
      Rails.logger.error("SandboxStartJob failed: #{e.message}\n#{e.backtrace.join("\n")}")
      sandbox.fail_job("Failed to start: #{e.message}")
      raise
    end
  end
end
```

> AGENT

Port 2201 is already allocated by Docker (from orphaned containers), but `assign_ssh_port` only checks the DB so it keeps picking 2201. The provision job needs to reassign the port using the Docker+TCP check right before creating the container. Let me fix this:

> TOOL

tool_use Read
id: toolu_013om7JrbVACwdj3bu87AxSU
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "offset": 575,
  "limit": 30
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_013om7JrbVACwdj3bu87AxSU
```
   575→    nil
   576→  end
   577→
   578→  def find_free_ssh_port
   579→    db_used = Sandbox.where.not(status: %w[destroyed stopped]).pluck(:ssh_port).compact
   580→    docker_used = docker_bound_ssh_ports
   581→    candidates = Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE.to_a - (db_used + docker_used).uniq
   582→    port = candidates.find { |p| port_free?(p) }
   583→    port || raise(Error, "No SSH ports available in range #{Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE}")
   584→  end
   585→
   586→  def docker_bound_ssh_ports
   587→    Docker::Container.all(all: true).flat_map do |c|
   588→      (c.info["Ports"] || []).map { |p| p["PublicPort"] }.compact
   589→    end.select { |p| Sandbox::SSH_PORT_RANGE.include?(p) }
   590→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError
   591→    []
   592→  end
   593→
   594→  # Verify a port is truly free by attempting a TCP connection to the Docker host.
   595→  # Returns true (free) if the connection is refused. Falls back to true if the
   596→  # Docker host is not reachable (e.g. production Linux without host.docker.internal).
   597→  def port_free?(port)
   598→    Timeout.timeout(0.3) { TCPSocket.new("host.docker.internal", port).close }
   599→    false # connected → port is occupied
   600→  rescue Errno::ECONNREFUSED, Timeout::Error
   601→    true  # refused/timed-out → port is free
   602→  rescue SocketError, Errno::EHOSTUNREACH, Errno::ENETUNREACH
   603→    true  # host not reachable → skip TCP check, rely on Docker API result
   604→  end
```

The `get.docker.com` script tries to install `ca-certificates` and `curl`, but Ubuntu 25.10 already has newer versions — causing the downgrade error. Replace the convenience script with the manual Docker apt repo setup, using `noble` as the codename fallback for Ubuntu versions Docker hasn't released packages for yet:

> AGENT

Two bugs: the `echo` with backslash continuation creates a malformed sources.list entry, and the `(subshell)` can't set variables in the outer shell. Since Ubuntu 25.10 is "questing" and Docker doesn't publish packages for it yet, just hardcode `noble`:

> AGENT

Build passes. The fix: instead of piping `get.docker.com | sh` (which re-installs `ca-certificates`/`curl` and fails when Ubuntu has newer versions), we add Docker's apt repo directly using the GPG key. Since Docker hasn't published packages for Ubuntu 25.10 ("questing") yet, we pin the repo to `noble` — the packages are forward-compatible.

`docker-compose.local.yml` is the file being run — it has its own cert approach (copies into volume via `init-traefik`) but uses `sandcastle.local` and the cert source is at `/tmp/sandcastle-traefik-certs`. Let me read it fully:

> AGENT

The problem: `docker-compose.local.yml` mounts certs from `/tmp/sandcastle-traefik-certs` which doesn't exist, so `init-traefik` fails to copy them and Traefik serves its default untrusted cert. Also the host is still `sandcastle.local`.

Fix `docker-compose.local.yml` to use `.cert` (same as we have for `docker-compose.dev.yml`) and `sandcastle.tc`:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01JWLDLSV8JvUbCSVERBvGnj
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/docker-compose.local.yml",
  "old_string": "      - /tmp/sandcastle-traefik-certs:/certs:ro",
  "new_string": "      - ./.cert:/certs:ro"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_01JWLDLSV8JvUbCSVERBvGnj

```json
  "new_string": "sandcastle.tc"
}
```
