## TLS/Certificate Configuration End-to-End Report

I've thoroughly explored the Sandcastle TLS configuration across the entire stack. Here's the complete picture:

### 1. INSTALLER CONFIGURATION (`installer/installer.sh.in`)

**TLS Mode Selection (Lines 118, 394, 732, 787)**
```bash
SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE="${SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE:-selfsigned}"
```
- Default is `selfsigned`
- Alternative mode: `letsencrypt` (requires ACME_EMAIL)

**Self-Signed Certificate Generation (Lines 817-824)**
- Only generates if mode is `selfsigned` AND cert doesn't exist
- Uses `openssl req -x509` with:
  - 4096-bit RSA key
  - 3650 days validity (10 years)
  - Subject: `/CN=$SANDCASTLE_HOST`
  - SubjectAltName: IP address of `$SANDCASTLE_HOST`
  - Output: `/data/traefik/certs/cert.pem` and `/data/traefik/certs/key.pem`

**Traefik Config Generation (Lines 827-930)**
- **Self-signed mode (Lines 827-854)**: Writes static TLS config with certificate file paths
- **LetsEncrypt mode (Lines 857-887)**: Configures ACME resolver with HTTP challenge
  ```yaml
  certificatesResolvers:
    letsencrypt:
      acme:
        email: ${ACME_EMAIL}
        storage: /data/acme.json
        httpChallenge:
          entryPoint: web
  ```

**Dynamic Rails Route (Lines 896-930)**
- **Self-signed**: Uses `HostRegexp` wildcard rule + `tls: {}`
- **LetsEncrypt**: Uses explicit `Host` rule + `tls: { certResolver: letsencrypt }`

### 2. ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES (`install-defaults` and `.env` file)

**Key Variables (Lines 29-42 in install-defaults)**
```bash
SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE="selfsigned"      # or "letsencrypt"
ACME_EMAIL="admin@example.com"        # Required for letsencrypt mode
SANDCASTLE_HTTP_PORT="80"
SANDCASTLE_HTTPS_PORT="443"
```

These are stored in:
- User config: `./sandcastle.env` (pre-install)
- Runtime: `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/.env` (generated, 600 perms)
- Installed config: `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/sandcastle.env`

### 3. DOCKER-COMPOSE CONFIGURATIONS

**Production (docker-compose.yml, Lines 49-50)**
```yaml
SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE: ${SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE:-letsencrypt}
SANDCASTLE_ALT_HOSTNAMES: ${SANDCASTLE_ALT_HOSTNAMES:-}
```
Note: Production defaults to `letsencrypt` if not set

**Development (docker-compose.dev.yml, Lines 114-115)**
```yaml
SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE: selfsigned
SANDCASTLE_TLS_CERT_DIR: /certs
```

**Local (docker-compose.local.yml, Lines 112)**
```yaml
SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE: selfsigned
```

**Traefik Volumes (across all compose files)**
```yaml
- ${DATA_MOUNT}/traefik/traefik.yml:/etc/traefik/traefik.yml:ro
- ${DATA_MOUNT}/traefik/dynamic:/data/dynamic:ro
- ${DATA_MOUNT}/traefik/acme.json:/data/acme.json        # LetsEncrypt
- ${DATA_MOUNT}/traefik/certs:/data/certs:ro             # Self-signed
```

### 4. ROUTE MANAGER (`app/services/route_manager.rb`)

**Rails Config Writing (Lines 79-117)**
- Called once at Rails startup via `config/initializers/traefik.rb`
- Uses `ENV["SANDCASTLE_HOST"]` and `SANDCASTLE_ALT_HOSTNAMES`
- Generates `rails.yml` in `/data/traefik/dynamic/`
- Calls `write_tls_config` to generate TLS configuration

**TLS Configuration Logic (Lines 121-142)**
```ruby
def write_tls_config
  if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
    cert_dir = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_TLS_CERT_DIR", "/data/certs")
    certs = [ { "certFile" => "#{cert_dir}/cert.pem", "keyFile" => "#{cert_dir}/key.pem" } ]
    
    if custom_cert_configured?  # Checks for custom-cert.pem and custom-key.pem
      certs << { "certFile" => "/data/certs/custom-cert.pem", "keyFile" => "/data/certs/custom-key.pem" }
    end
    
    File.write(tls_path, { "tls" => { "certificates" => certs } }.to_yaml)
  else
    File.delete(tls_path) if File.exist?(tls_path)  # Let certResolver handle it
  end
end
```

**Sandbox Route TLS Config (Lines 174-180)**
```ruby
def tls_config
  if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
    {}  # Uses default Traefik certs
  else
    { "certResolver" => "letsencrypt" }
  end
end
```

### 5. TRAEFIK INITIALIZATION

**Static Config (`traefik.yml`)** - Generated by installer
- Self-signed mode: Static TLS section with cert files
- LetsEncrypt mode: `certificatesResolvers` section with ACME config

**Dynamic Config (`/data/traefik/dynamic/*.yml`)** - Generated by Rails
- `rails.yml`: Main Rails routes
- `tls.yml`: TLS certificate definitions (self-signed only)
- `sandbox-*.yml`: Per-sandbox routes with TLS config

### 6. CERTIFICATE GENERATION SCRIPTS

**Installer (built-in, Lines 820-823)**
```bash
openssl req -x509 -newkey rsa:4096 -sha256 -days 3650 -nodes \
  -keyout "$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/key.pem" -out "$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/cert.pem" \
  -subj "/CN=$SANDCASTLE_HOST" \
  -addext "subjectAltName=IP:$SANDCASTLE_HOST"
```

**Local Development (`scripts/generate-local-cert.sh`)**
- Uses `mkcert` for locally-trusted certificates
- Generates for: `sandcastle.local`, `*.sandcastle.local`, `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`
- Installs CA in system keychain automatically
- Output: `/tmp/sandcastle-traefik-certs/`

### 7. INITIALIZATION FLOW

The TLS setup flow is:

1. **Install time**
   - User specifies `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE` in `sandcastle.env`
   - Installer generates `traefik.yml` based on mode
   - If `selfsigned`: Generate self-signed cert with `openssl`
   - If `letsencrypt`: Configure ACME with email, wait for Let's Encrypt challenge

2. **Runtime (Rails startup)**
   - `config/initializers/traefik.rb` fires `after_initialize`
   - Calls `RouteManager.new.write_rails_config(host: SANDCASTLE_HOST)`
   - Generates `/data/traefik/dynamic/rails.yml` with appropriate TLS config
   - Generates `/data/traefik/dynamic/tls.yml` if self-signed mode

3. **Traefik detection**
   - Traefik watches `/data/traefik/dynamic/` for YAML changes
   - Reads `traefik.yml` for static config (entrypoints, resolvers)
   - Reads `*.yml` in dynamic/ for routes and TLS

### 8. KEY ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES REFERENCE

| Variable | Default | Mode | Used By |
|----------|---------|------|---------|
| `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE` | `selfsigned` (installer), `letsencrypt` (prod) | Both | Installer, RouteManager, Traefik |
| `SANDCASTLE_HOST` | Auto-detected | Both | Installer, RouteManager, cert CN |
| `SANDCASTLE_ALT_HOSTNAMES` | Empty | Both | RouteManager (adds alt Host rules) |
| `SANDCASTLE_TLS_CERT_DIR` | `/data/certs` | Self-signed only | RouteManager |
| `ACME_EMAIL` | Optional | LetsEncrypt | Installer, Traefik |
| `SANDCASTLE_HTTP_PORT` | 80 | Both | Firewall, Traefik entrypoint |
| `SANDCASTLE_HTTPS_PORT` | 443 | Both | Firewall, Traefik entrypoint |

### 9. CUSTOM CERTIFICATE SUPPORT

RouteManager checks for custom certificates:
- Path: `/data/traefik/certs/custom-cert.pem` and `/data/traefik/certs/custom-key.pem`
- If present, adds them as secondary certificate option in `tls.yml`
- Allows serving multiple certificates for different domains

This provides a clear two-mode TLS strategy with full flexibility for custom certificates and alternative hostnames.

## Summary of TLS Configuration in Sandcastle

I've gathered all the detailed TLS-related content from your Sandcastle project. Here's what I found:

### 1. **OpenSSL Self-Signed Certificate Command** (Lines 820-823, installer/installer.sh.in)

```bash
openssl req -x509 -newkey rsa:4096 -sha256 -days 3650 -nodes \
  -keyout "$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/key.pem" -out "$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/cert.pem" \
  -subj "/CN=$SANDCASTLE_HOST" \
  -addext "subjectAltName=IP:$SANDCASTLE_HOST" 2>/dev/null
```

**Key details:**
- Algorithm: RSA 4096-bit
- Hash: SHA256
- Validity: 3650 days (10 years)
- No passphrase (`-nodes`)
- Uses IP as SubjectAltName
- Only generated if cert doesn't already exist (`[ ! -f "$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/cert.pem" ]`)

---

### 2. **Traefik Configuration - Self-Signed Mode** (Lines 827-854)

```yaml
entryPoints:
  web:
    address: ":80"
    http:
      redirections:
        entryPoint:
          to: websecure
          scheme: https
  websecure:
    address: ":443"

providers:
  file:
    directory: /data/dynamic
    watch: true

tls:
  certificates:
    - certFile: /data/certs/cert.pem
      keyFile: /data/certs/key.pem

log:
  level: INFO

api:
  dashboard: false
```

---

### 3. **Traefik Configuration - Let's Encrypt Mode** (Lines 857-887)

```yaml
entryPoints:
  web:
    address: ":80"
    http:
      redirections:
        entryPoint:
          to: websecure
          scheme: https
  websecure:
    address: ":443"

certificatesResolvers:
  letsencrypt:
    acme:
      email: "${ACME_EMAIL}"
      storage: /data/acme.json
      httpChallenge:
        entryPoint: web

providers:
  file:
    directory: /data/dynamic
    watch: true

log:
  level: INFO

api:
  dashboard: false
```

---

### 4. **Dynamic Router Config - Self-Signed** (Lines 896-911)

```yaml
http:
  routers:
    rails:
      rule: "HostRegexp(\`.+\`)"
      service: rails
      entryPoints:
        - websecure
      tls: {}
  services:
    rails:
      loadBalancer:
        servers:
          - url: "http://sandcastle-web:80"
```

---

### 5. **Dynamic Router Config - Let's Encrypt** (Lines 912-928)

```yaml
http:
  routers:
    rails:
      rule: "Host(\`${SANDCASTLE_HOST}\`)"
      service: rails
      entryPoints:
        - websecure
      tls:
        certResolver: letsencrypt
  services:
    rails:
      loadBalancer:
        servers:
          - url: "http://sandcastle-web:80"
```

---

### 6. **TLS Mode Detection & Logic** (Lines 118, 732, 787, 817, 896)

The flow in `installer.sh.in`:

1. **Line 118**: Default `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE="${SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE:-selfsigned}"`
2. **Line 732**: Stored in runtime `.env` file: `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE="${SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE}"`
3. **Line 787**: Also written to installed config: `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE="${SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE}"`
4. **Line 817**: Checked to decide certificate generation: `if [ "$SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE" = "selfsigned" ]`
5. **Line 896**: Checked again for router configuration: `if [ "$SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE" = "selfsigned" ]`

---

### 7. **install-defaults** - TLS Variables (Lines 34-38)

```bash
# TLS mode: "letsencrypt" or "selfsigned"
SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE="selfsigned"

# Email for Let's Encrypt (required when TLS mode is letsencrypt)
# ACME_EMAIL="admin@example.com"
```

---

### 8. **docker-compose.yml** - Traefik Service & Volumes (Lines 1-14)

```yaml
traefik:
  image: traefik:v3.6.8
  restart: unless-stopped
  ports:
    - "80:80"
    - "443:443"
  volumes:
    - /data/traefik/traefik.yml:/etc/traefik/traefik.yml:ro
    - /data/traefik/dynamic:/data/dynamic:ro
    - /data/traefik/acme.json:/data/acme.json
    - /data/traefik/certs:/data/certs:ro
  networks:
    - sandcastle-web
```

---

### 9. **mkcert Support** - Three Files

#### **A. scripts/generate-local-cert.sh** (Full content)
```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

# Generate locally-trusted certificate for sandcastle.local using mkcert
# This certificate will be automatically trusted by your browser

CERT_DIR="/tmp/sandcastle-traefik-certs"

if ! command -v mkcert &> /dev/null; then
  echo "Error: mkcert is not installed"
  echo "Install with: brew install mkcert"
  echo "Then run: mkcert -install"
  exit 1
fi

# Ensure mkcert CA is installed in system keychain
if ! mkcert -CAROOT &> /dev/null; then
  echo "Installing mkcert CA in system keychain..."
  mkcert -install
fi

echo "Generating locally-trusted certificate for sandcastle.local..."
mkdir -p "$CERT_DIR"

# Generate certificate for sandcastle.local
cd "$CERT_DIR"
mkcert \
  -cert-file cert.pem \
  -key-file key.pem \
  sandcastle.local \
  "*.sandcastle.local" \
  localhost \
  127.0.0.1 \
  ::1

echo ""
echo "✓ Certificate generated at: $CERT_DIR"
echo "  - cert.pem (certificate)"
echo "  - key.pem (private key)"
echo ""
echo "The certificate is now trusted by your system."
echo "Restart Traefik with: mise run deploy:local"
```

#### **B. docs/LOCAL_CERT_SETUP.md** - Documents mkcert setup

Key points:
- Uses `mkcert -install` to add CA to system keychain
- Generates certs for: `sandcastle.local`, `*.sandcastle.local`, `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1`
- Certs placed in `/tmp/sandcastle-traefik-certs/`
- Verification includes checking Traefik dynamic config: `/data/dynamic/rails.yml`

#### **C. app/views/pages/guide.html.erb** (Lines 247-253)

Includes mkcert install instructions in the user guide:
```bash
# Install mkcert (macOS example)
brew install mkcert
mkcert -install

# Generate certificate (adjust domains as needed)
mkcert sandcastle.internal sc.local
```

---

### 10. **Key TLS Configuration Files & Directories**

```
$SANDCASTLE_HOME/
├── data/
│   └── traefik/
│       ├── traefik.yml          (Main Traefik config - generated by installer)
│       ├── acme.json            (Let's Encrypt state - created empty if needed)
│       ├── certs/
│       │   ├── cert.pem         (Self-signed cert - generated if TLS_MODE=selfsigned)
│       │   └── key.pem          (Private key - generated if TLS_MODE=selfsigned)
│       └── dynamic/
│           └── rails.yml        (Dynamic router config - generated by installer)
```

---

### Summary of TLS Modes

| Aspect | Self-Signed | Let's Encrypt |
|--------|------------|--------------|
| **Config Section** | Lines 827-854 | Lines 857-887 |
| **Router Rule** | `HostRegexp(.+)` (all hosts) | `Host(${SANDCASTLE_HOST})` |
| **TLS Setting** | `tls: {}` (default cert) | `tls: certResolver: letsencrypt` |
| **Certificate Source** | `/data/certs/{cert,key}.pem` | Traefik ACME resolver |
| **ACME Config** | None | Uses `${ACME_EMAIL}`, `/data/acme.json` |
| **Requires DNS** | No (IP-based) | Yes (HTTP-01 challenge) |

The project includes **optional** `mkcert` support (for local development with browser-trusted certs):

- Docs: `REDACTED.md`

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/.claude/plans/synchronous-fluttering-lagoon.md",
  "content": "# Plan: Add `mkcert` TLS Mode\n\n## Context\n\nCurrently Sandcastle has two TLS modes:\n- **`selfsigned`** — `openssl` generates a self-signed cert. Traefik serves it from `/data/certs/cert.pem`. Browser always shows an \"untrusted\" warning.\n- **`letsencrypt`** — ACME via Let's Encrypt. Requires a public domain and ACME email. Not usable on private networks.\n\nThere's a gap: users on private networks or homelabs who want browser-trusted HTTPS without a public domain. **mkcert** fills this gap — it creates a local CA, signs certs with it, and if the CA is installed on client machines, the browser trusts the cert with no warnings. The cert format is identical to selfsigned (PEM files), so Traefik config doesn't change.\n\nThe user has been doing this manually (running mkcert on dev machine, copying certs into compose files). The goal is to make `mkcert` a first-class installer mode.\n\n## Files to Modify\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `installer/installer.sh.in` | Add mkcert install + cert generation branch |\n| `install-defaults` | Document new `mkcert` TLS mode option |\n| `app/services/route_manager.rb` | Treat `mkcert` same as `selfsigned` |\n| `installer.sh` | Regenerate from `.in` file |\n\n## Implementation\n\n### 1. `install-defaults`\n\nAdd `mkcert` to the TLS mode docs:\n\n```bash\n# TLS mode: \"letsencrypt\", \"selfsigned\", or \"mkcert\"\n#   letsencrypt - automatic cert via ACME (requires public domain + ACME_EMAIL)\n#   selfsigned  - self-signed cert via openssl (browser shows warning)\n#   mkcert      - locally-trusted cert via mkcert (install CA on client machines\n#                 for browser trust; ideal for private networks / homelabs)\nSANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE=\"selfsigned\"\n```\n\n### 2. `installer/installer.sh.in`\n\n**Add `install_mkcert` helper** (near other install helpers):\n\n```bash\ninstall_mkcert() {\n  if command -v mkcert &>/dev/null; then\n    return 0\n  fi\n  info \"Installing mkcert...\"\n  ARCH=$(uname -m)\n  case \"$ARCH\" in\n    x86_64)  MKCERT_ARCH=\"amd64\" ;;\n    aarch64) MKCERT_ARCH=\"arm64\" ;;\n    *)       die \"Unsupported architecture for mkcert: $ARCH\" ;;\n  esac\n  curl -fsSL \"https://dl.filippo.io/mkcert/latest?for=linux/${MKCERT_ARCH}\" \\\n    -o /usr/local/bin/mkcert\n  chmod +x /usr/local/bin/mkcert\n}\n```\n\n**Add `mkcert` branch to cert generation** (currently at the `if [ \"$SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE\" = \"selfsigned\" ]` block, around line 817):\n\n```bash\nif [ \"$SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE\" = \"selfsigned\" ]; then\n  if [ ! -f \"$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/cert.pem\" ]; then\n    info \"Generating self-signed certificate for $SANDCASTLE_HOST...\"\n    openssl req -x509 -newkey rsa:4096 -sha256 -days 3650 -nodes \\\n      -keyout \"$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/key.pem\" \\\n      -out \"$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/cert.pem\" \\\n      -subj \"/CN=$SANDCASTLE_HOST\" \\\n      -addext \"subjectAltName=IP:$SANDCASTLE_HOST\" 2>/dev/null\n  fi\nelif [ \"$SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE\" = \"mkcert\" ]; then\n  install_mkcert\n  # Create CA and generate cert (idempotent)\n  CAROOT=\"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/traefik/certs\"\n  CAROOT=\"$CAROOT\" mkcert -install 2>/dev/null || true\n  if [ ! -f \"$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/cert.pem\" ]; then\n    info \"Generating mkcert certificate for $SANDCASTLE_HOST...\"\n    CAROOT=\"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/traefik/certs\" \\\n      mkcert \\\n        -cert-file \"$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/cert.pem\" \\\n        -key-file  \"$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/key.pem\" \\\n        \"$SANDCASTLE_HOST\" \"*.${SANDCASTLE_HOST}\" localhost 127.0.0.1 ::1\n  fi\n  # Copy CA cert to a well-known location for client distribution\n  cp \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/traefik/certs/rootCA.pem\" \\\n     \"$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/rootCA.pem\" 2>/dev/null || true\n  info \"mkcert CA root: $SANDCASTLE_HOME/data/traefik/certs/rootCA.pem\"\n  info \"Install on client machines to trust this server's certificate:\"\n  info \"  macOS:   sudo security add-trusted-cert -d -r trustRoot -k /Library/Keychains/System.keychain rootCA.pem\"\n  info \"  Linux:   sudo cp rootCA.pem /usr/local/share/ca-certificates/sandcastle.crt && sudo update-ca-certificates\"\nfi\n```\n\n**Key design decisions:**\n- Use `CAROOT` env var to store the CA inside `$SANDCASTLE_HOME` (persistent across reinstalls, not in `/root/.local/share/mkcert` which could be wiped)\n- Cert generation is idempotent (skipped if cert already exists)\n- Traefik static config for `mkcert` is **identical to `selfsigned`** (both use file-based certs at `/data/certs/cert.pem`) — no new `traefik.yml` section needed\n- `rootCA.pem` is saved to `$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/` so it's accessible via the Traefik certs volume\n\n**Update the Traefik static config generation block** to include `mkcert` alongside `selfsigned`:\n\n```bash\nif [ \"$SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE\" = \"selfsigned\" ] || [ \"$SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE\" = \"mkcert\" ]; then\n  # ... existing selfsigned traefik.yml heredoc ...\nfi\n```\n\nSame for the dynamic `rails.yml` initial write.\n\n### 3. `app/services/route_manager.rb`\n\n`mkcert` certs are file-based just like `selfsigned` — treat them identically:\n\n```ruby\n# write_tls_config\nSELFSIGNED_MODES = %w[selfsigned mkcert].freeze\n\ndef write_tls_config\n  tls_path = File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, \"tls.yml\")\n  if SELFSIGNED_MODES.include?(ENV[\"SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE\"])\n    cert_dir = ENV.fetch(\"SANDCASTLE_TLS_CERT_DIR\", \"/data/certs\")\n    certs = [ { \"certFile\" => \"#{cert_dir}/cert.pem\", \"keyFile\" => \"#{cert_dir}/key.pem\" } ]\n    if custom_cert_configured?\n      certs << { \"certFile\" => \"/data/certs/custom-cert.pem\", \"keyFile\" => \"/data/certs/custom-key.pem\" }\n    end\n    File.write(tls_path, { \"tls\" => { \"certificates\" => certs } }.to_yaml)\n  else\n    File.delete(tls_path) if File.exist?(tls_path)\n  end\nend\n\n# tls_config (for sandbox routes)\ndef tls_config\n  if SELFSIGNED_MODES.include?(ENV[\"SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE\"])\n    {}\n  else\n    { \"certResolver\" => \"letsencrypt\" }\n  end\nend\n```\n\n### 4. Regenerate `installer.sh`\n\n```bash\n# From project root\nbash installer/build.sh   # or however installer.sh is built from .in\n```\n\nNeed to check how `installer.sh` is regenerated — look for a build script or Makefile target.\n\n## Verification\n\n1. Set `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE=mkcert` in test env\n2. Run installer — verify mkcert is installed, cert is generated at `$TRAEFIK_DIR/certs/cert.pem`\n3. Confirm `rootCA.pem` is present alongside the cert\n4. Confirm Traefik loads the cert without errors\n5. On a client machine: copy `rootCA.pem`, install it, navigate to the site — should show trusted HTTPS\n6. Test that `RouteManager` writes correct `tls.yml` when `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE=mkcert`\n7. Verify `selfsigned` and `letsencrypt` modes still work unchanged\n\n## Open Question\n\nHow is `installer.sh` regenerated from `installer.sh.in`? Need to check for a build script before executing.\n"

```
     1→#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2→# Build installer.sh from installer.sh.in template
     3→set -euo pipefail
     4→
     5→SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
     6→REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/.." && pwd)"
     7→TEMPLATES_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/templates"
     8→TEMPLATE_FILE="$SCRIPT_DIR/installer.sh.in"
     9→OUTPUT_FILE="$REPO_ROOT/installer.sh"
    10→
    11→# Colors for output
    12→RED='\033[0;31m'
    13→GREEN='\033[0;32m'
    14→YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
    15→NC='\033[0m' # No Color
    16→
    17→info() { echo -e "${GREEN}→${NC} $*" >&2; }
    18→warn() { echo -e "${YELLOW}⚠${NC} $*" >&2; }
    19→error() { echo -e "${RED}✗${NC} $*" >&2; }
    20→ok() { echo -e "${GREEN}✓${NC} $*" >&2; }
    21→
    22→if [ ! -f "$TEMPLATE_FILE" ]; then
    23→  error "Template file not found: $TEMPLATE_FILE"
    24→  error "Run: cp installer.sh installer/installer.sh.in"
    25→  error "Then replace heredocs with @@TEMPLATE:filename@@ markers"
    26→  exit 1
    27→fi
    28→
    29→info "Building installer.sh from template..."
    30→
    31→# Create temporary output file
    32→TEMP_OUTPUT=$(mktemp)
    33→
    34→# Process the template line by line
    35→while IFS= read -r line; do
    36→  # Check for template markers: @@TEMPLATE:filename@@
    37→  if [[ "$line" =~ @@TEMPLATE:([^@]+)@@ ]]; then
    38→    template_file="${BASH_REMATCH[1]}"
    39→
    40→    # Determine the full path to the template
    41→    if [[ "$template_file" == /* ]]; then
    42→      # Absolute path from repo root
    43→      full_path="$REPO_ROOT/${template_file#/}"
    44→    elif [[ "$template_file" == templates/* ]]; then
    45→      # Relative to installer/templates/
    46→      full_path="$TEMPLATES_DIR/${template_file#templates/}"
    47→    else
    48→      # Assume it's in templates/
    49→      full_path="$TEMPLATES_DIR/$template_file"
    50→    fi
    51→
    52→    if [ ! -f "$full_path" ]; then
    53→      error "Template file not found: $full_path"
    54→      rm -f "$TEMP_OUTPUT"
    55→      exit 1
    56→    fi
    57→
    58→    info "  Injecting: $template_file"
    59→    cat "$full_path"
    60→  else
    61→    # Regular line, output as-is
    62→    echo "$line"
    63→  fi
    64→done < "$TEMPLATE_FILE" > "$TEMP_OUTPUT"
    65→
    66→# Move to final location
    67→mv "$TEMP_OUTPUT" "$OUTPUT_FILE"
    68→chmod +x "$OUTPUT_FILE"
    69→
    70→ok "Built: $OUTPUT_FILE"
    71→echo ""
    72→info "Verify the output and test before committing!"
    73→
